After all these events transpired – the Sivayogi, intending to alleviate the suffering of the cows, performed parakaya pravesham and led the cows to their homes in the village in the cowherd's body; the cowherd's wife came to touch him, but he rejected her; he stayed in a public monastery; and the villagers gathered, saw the Yogi's state, and advised the woman – Thirumoola Deva Nayanar, who was in the public monastery, rose from there and followed the path the herds of cows had taken. He follows the path the cows took, intending to find his body and re-enter it. He does not find his previous body, which he had kept safely. When he goes to the place where his previous body was, he does not find it. Here, the divine Sekkizhar Peruman refers to the body as "udal porai" (burden of the body). That is, it means the burden that is the body. For Yogis who seek liberation, even the body appears as a burden. Therefore, the divine Sekkizhar Peruman refers to it as a burden. And Thirukkural says, "Thodarpaadu evankol pirapparukkalutraarkku udambum migai" (What connection is there for those who seek to cut off birth? Even the body is superfluous). Seeing that his previous body was missing, Thirumoola Deva Nayanar, who possessed a mind that had attained complete true knowledge, understands and realizes. "Muzhuthunarntha meignana sinthai" (mind that has attained complete true knowledge) came to Thirumoola Deva Nayanar through the spiritual instruction of Nandiperuman's grace. When he investigates with that, he realizes. What does he realize? He realizes the truth that Lord Shiva, out of divine grace, concealed that body with the intention that the meaning of the 28 Agamas revealed by Him, which includes the four paths of Charya, Kriya, Yoga, and Gnana, should be expounded in Tamil through the words of Thirumoolar in this earthly world. Lord Shiva revealed the Kamikam and other 28 Agamas from His five sacred faces, such as Sadyojatam, which are part of His Ishana face. The meaning of these Agamas includes the four paths of Charya, Kriya, Yoga, and Gnana, the certainty of the three categories (Pati, Pasu, Pasam), the means, and the fruit. From the five sacred faces of Maha Sadashiva Murthy's Ishana face, namely Sadyojatam, Vamadevam, Aghoram, Tatpurusham, and Ishanam, the Lord revealed the Agamas. From the Sadyojatam face, five Agamas: Kamikam, Yogajam, Chintyam, Karanam, Ajitam. From the Vamadevam face, five Agamas: Diptam, Sukshmam, Sahasram, Amshuman, Suprabhedam. From the Aghoram face, five Agamas: Vijayam, Nisvasam, Svayambhavam, Agneyam, Veeram. From the Tatpurusham face, five Agamas: Rauravam, Makutam, Vimalam, Chandrajnanam, Mukhabimbam. From the Ishanam face, eight Agamas: Protkeetam, Lalitam, Siddham, Santanam, Sarvoktam, Paramesvaram, Kiranam, Vatulam. Thus, Lord Shiva revealed a total of 28 Agamas. Thirumandiram speaks of this as "Anjaa mugathil arumporul kettathe" (the rare truth heard from the fearless face). The Vedas speak of this as "Ishana Sarva Vidyanam". Thiruvachagam speaks of this as "Matravai thammai Mahendirathu irunthu utra aimugangalal panithu aruliyum" (and also by bestowing from the five faces in Mahendra) and "Mahendira madanil sonna Agamam" (the Agamas spoken in Mahendra). These 28 Agamas were revealed for the Sivagotra Rishis such as Kausikar, Kashyapar, Bharadvajar, Gautamar, and Agastiyar. These Agamas were revealed for those who are superior in knowledge, such as Rishis and great sages. To enable the people of this earthly world to understand these Agamas according to their maturity and attain salvation, the Lord, intending to expound these Agamas in Tamil through the words of Thirumoolar, thus concealed Thirumoola Deva Nayanar's old body. Thirumandiram states: "Anjana meni arivaiyor paagathan Anjodu irupathu ulla Agamam Anjali kuppi arupathu aruvarum Anjaa mugathil arumporul kettathe." (The one with a dark body, with wisdom as half, the Agamas numbering twenty-five, sixty-six bowed with folded hands and heard the rare truth from the fearless face.) Saiva Samaya Neri states: "Arupathu aruvar pranavarathi arivura kettar Agamam" (Sixty-six, starting with Pranava, heard the Agamas with understanding). We saw that Lord Shiva instructed the Agamas to the Goddess, saying, "Vellimal varai Kayilaiyil veetriruntharuli Thulluvar punal veniyar aruludaiyal endru" (Residing in the silver mountain Kayilai, the Lord with flowing water in His matted hair, out of grace). So, these Agamas were revealed for those who are superior in knowledge. The Lord intended that the same meaning should come in Tamil through the words of Thirumoolar for the people of this earthly world. That is why, to indicate that Thirumandiram contains the essence of the Agamas, the divine Sekkizhar Peruman composed this Puranam of Thirumoola Deva Nayanar in 28 Thiruvirutthams. We recall this point here. The Lord intended to expound it in Tamil. Therefore, it is understood that the Agamas already revealed by the Lord were published in Sanskrit, not in Tamil. Thirumoola Deva Nayanar, through his true realization, understands and clarifies this act of the Lord concealing his previous body due to this divine intention. Therefore, Thirumoola Deva Nayanar realizes that concealing the body and expounding the meaning of the Agamas in Tamil are both part of the divine intention. That is why Thirumoola Deva Nayanar states in Thirumandiram: "Ennai nandraaga Iraivan padaithanan Thannai nandraaga Tamil seiyumarē." (The Lord created me well, so that I may do Tamil well for Him.) The 24th Thiruppadal: "Sutriya akkullathoor thodarntharkku thodarbinmai Mutrave mozhintharula avar meendu ponatharpin Petran meethu uyarthavar thal sinthithu perugu aarvach Chetra muthal kadinthavar tham Aavaduthanthurai sernthar." At this time, the relatives of that Moolan, the cowherd community, follow Thirumoola Deva Nayanar, thinking that one of their own has become like this. Thirumoola Deva Nayanar explains the truth of the sacred history that transpired and enlightens them. He makes them understand the truth that there is no connection between them and him. They then return. After they leave, Thirumoola Deva Nayanar is one whose nature is to contemplate only the sacred feet of the Lord. Thirumoola Deva Nayanar, who had completely eradicated desire, anger, and other passions, proceeds to the sacred temple of Thiruvaavaduthurai. "Aa" means pasutvam (animalistic nature). "Adu" means to destroy. "Aa adu thurai" means the place where the pasutvam of beings is destroyed. To indicate that it is a cool place without the heat of karma because karma is destroyed there, the divine Sekkizhar Peruman refers to it as "Aavaduthanthurai". Earlier, Thirumoola Deva Nayanar bathed in the Kaveri and worshipped the Lord of Thiruvaavaduthurai. Now, he comes to Thiruvaavaduthurai again and stands worshipping the Lord. The 25th Thiruppadal: "Aavaduthanthurai anainthu angu arumporulai ura vanangi Mevuvar purakkudapal mikku uyarnda arasin keezh Deva irukkai amarndharuli Sivayogam thalainindru Poovalarum ithayaththup porulodum punarnthirundhar." He reaches Thiruvaavaduthurai temple, worships Lord Shiva, and stays there. In the outer circumambulatory path of the temple, on the western side, there is a very tall Pipal tree. This Pipal tree was on the western side of the outer circumambulatory path of the temple at that time. It seems that it must have been to the west of the place where Thirumoolar's shrine is now in Thiruvaavaduthurai temple. This is a very dense and tall Pipal tree. The Pipal tree under which Thirumoola Deva Nayanar sat in yoga, as sung by the divine Sekkizhar Peruman, is not there now. Now, there is a Pipal tree on the eastern side of the temple, near the eastern gopuram. It belongs to a variety of Pipal tree called "Padar Arasu". This "Padar Arasu" tree, after a few centuries, falls down and takes root. At that time, the old tree and its roots also perish. Thus, we see this Pipal tree, which was originally in the west, coming around the temple and now being located near the eastern entrance. This is the current state of that "Padar Arasu" tree. The Pipal tree under which Thirumoola Deva Nayanar sat in yoga, which was outside the outer circumambulatory path to the west of the temple, is not found now due to our lack of penance. Under that Pipal tree, Thirumoola Deva Nayanar sits on a seat called "Deva Irukkai" or "Devasanam". It is called "Yogasanam". This is a divine seat. If we see the idol of Thirumoolar established in Thirumoola Deva Nayanar's shrine now, we can understand that seat. Sitting thus, he remains in Sivayoga, united with the precious Lord who resides in his heart. To be in Sivayoga means to firmly establish one's intellect in the Sivagnanam (divine knowledge) of Lord Shiva; that is called Sivayoga. Being in such a state, Thirumoola Deva Nayanar is inseparably united with Lord Shiva, the true essence, who resides within the lotus of his heart, which blossoms like a flower. The 26th and 27th Thiruppadalgals: "Oon udambin piravi vidam theernthu ulagaththor uyya Gnana muthal nangu malar natthirumandira maalai Panmai murai or aandukku ondraaga param porulam ena eiyiru anintharai ondru avan than ena eduthu munniya apporul maalai Tamil moovayiram saathi Manniya moovayirathu aandu ippuvimel magizhndhirunthu Senni madhi aninthartham thiruvarulaal Thirukkayilai Thannil anainthu orukalum piriyamai thal adainthar." These are the Thiruppadalgals. For the beings that have come to this earthly world, to remove the poison of this fleshly birth and for all beings in this world to attain salvation, Thirumoola Deva Nayanar bestows the sacred garland of Thirumandiram. That garland of Thirumandiram is the garland of Thirumandiram that elaborates on the four paths, starting with Gnana. He bestows this garland of Thirumandiram as 3000 mantras, staying for 3000 years, at the rate of one mantra per year. He began this garland of Thirumandiram by praising the Lord as "Ondru avan thane" (He is one). Thirumoola Deva Nayanar bestows this according to the divine decree established by the Lord. We understand that it is the Lord's decree that one Thirumandiram was revealed per year. Thirumoola Deva Nayanar was one who had mastered Sivayoga. Those who have mastered Sivayoga will be omniscient, having conquered time. For such people, when they are in yogic trance, one year will pass like a moment. In such a state, Thirumoola Deva Nayanar bestowed this garland of Thirumandiram. One Thirumandiram per year. So, he bestowed 3000 Thirumandirams over 3000 years. We find internal evidence for this in Thirumandiram itself. He bestows it according to the divine decree established by the Lord. We understand that it is the Lord's decree that one Thirumandiram was revealed per year. Thirumoola Deva Nayanar was one who had mastered Sivayoga. Those who have mastered Sivayoga will be omniscient, having conquered time. For such people, when they are in yogic trance, one year will pass like a moment. In such a state, Thirumoola Deva Nayanar bestowed this garland of Thirumandiram. One Thirumandiram per year. So, he bestowed 3000 Thirumandirams over 3000 years. We find internal evidence for this in Thirumandiram itself.

The 80th Thiruppadal states: "Ikkāyaththil eṇṇili kōṭi irunthēṉ irāppakal aṟṟa iṭaththē" (I resided in this body for countless crores, in a place beyond day and night). The 82nd Thiruppadal also states: "Gnānaththalaivi than nandi nagarpukku ūnamil oṉpathu kōṭi yugam thannuḷ gnānappālāṭṭi nāthanai archiththu yāṉum irunthēṉ aṟpōthiyiṉ kīḻē" (Entering the city of Nandi, the leader of wisdom, I too resided under the Pipal tree, worshipping the Lord with the milk of wisdom for nine crores of yugas without blemish). Here, "nine crores" signifies countless. He composed Thirumandiram in such a way that the four principles, starting with Gnana, are included. The four principles starting with Gnana are Gnana, Yoga, Kriya, and Charya, which are the four paths of Saivism. Placing Gnana first is the order of creation. Sivagnana Siddhiyar states: "Sankaranaai adaiyum nanmaarkkam naalavaithaan Gnana Yoga Narkriya Chariyai ena navitruvathum Saivar" (The good paths to attain Shankara are Gnana, Yoga, good Kriya, and Charya, as declared by Saivites). We also saw in Periya Puranam, Thiruppadal 3591, "Nalan sirantha Gnana Yoga Kriya Chariyaiyellam" (All the excellent Gnana, Yoga, Kriya, and Charya). Therefore, placing Charya first as Charya, Kriya, Yoga, Gnana is one method. Placing Gnana first as Gnana, Yoga, Kriya, Charya is the order of creation. The Gnana Guru is the Guru for all four. Therefore, here, when narrating this sacred history, the divine Sekkizhar Peruman, in the context of the Guru who came to instruct Gnana, places Gnana first and the others in sequence. "Mantra Malai" (Garland of Mantras) is the name Thirumoola Deva Nayanar gave to this sacred text. The divine Sekkizhar Peruman refers to it as "Nattrirumandira Malai" (Excellent Sacred Garland of Mantras). Goodness, in its highest form, is Moksha, liberation. Therefore, for those who desire Moksha, for students who desire Moksha, this Agama was revealed to instruct them, hence the divine Sekkizhar Peruman refers to it as "Nattrirumandira Malai". The divine Sekkizhar Peruman shows us that Thirumoola Deva Nayanar begins it with "Ondru avan thane" (He is one). The first Thiruppadal of Thirumandiram is: "Ondru avan thane irandu avan innarul nindranan Moondrinul nangu unarnthan ainthu vendranan Aaru virinthanan ezhum par sendranan than irunthan unarnthu ette." (He is one, His sweet grace stood as two, He understood four within three, He conquered five, He expanded as six, He went beyond seven, He remained as eight, having understood.) In this, the divine Sekkizhar Peruman states that he begins this garland of Thirumandiram with "Ondru avan thane". This is the beginning of the first Thiruppadal of the Thirumandiram garland composed by Thirumoola Deva Nayanar. The invocation to Ganesha, "Ainthu karathanai aanai mugathanai Indhin ilampirai polum eyitranai Nandi magathanai gnanak kozhunthinai Sinthaiyil vaithu adi potrugindrene" (I worship the feet of the five-handed, elephant-faced, tusked like the young crescent moon, son of Nandi, the sprout of wisdom, by placing Him in my mind), is a prayer to Ganesha performed outside the book, known as "kaappu" (protection). "Ondru avan thane" is the place where the book properly begins. Therefore, the divine Sekkizhar Peruman states this. This entire garland of Thirumandiram is a praise of Lord Shiva. Since it speaks of the certainty of the three categories (Pati, Pasu, Pasam), which are the essence of Sivagama, for the salvation of the world, and the ways for beings to attain Lord Shiva, and the attainment itself, and the resulting bliss of Sivananda, this book, the garland of Thirumandiram, begins with "Upadesam" (instruction) and concludes in the ninth Tantra with the section "Stotram Sarva Vyapi" (Praise, All-pervading) with "Perum paraporan Pinjagan emmirai Oorum sakalan uzhappilithane" (The great transcendent, the one with matted hair, our Lord, the all-pervading, the tireless one) and "Vazhgave vazhga mayamilan padame" (Long live, long live the feet of the faultless one). Through this, we see that it becomes a garland of Thirumandiram offered to Lord Shiva. Thirumoola Deva Nayanar says, "Kootruththaanai yaan koorugindrene" (I speak of the one who kicked Death). This is revealed to us as the central theme of the garland of Thirumandiram. We understand from the idea "Perum paraporan" at the end of the book that Lord Shiva is the Supreme Being, beyond all things. Thus, the history of Thirumoola Deva Nayanar composing the garland of Thirumandiram is narrated by the divine Sekkizhar Peruman. Now, the editor elaborately explains the earlier part of Thirumoola Deva Nayanar's life and the greatness of the garland of Thirumandiram. We will continue to contemplate that.

Chapter 07 — ThiruMoola Deva Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Periya Puranam, Part 906, by the divine Sekkizhar Peruman. Vambaraa Varivandu Sarukkam. Thirumoola Deva Nayanar Puranam. In the concluding part of the commentary on Thirumoola Deva Nayanar Puranam, the editor has included a section containing very rare information about Thirumandiram, authored by Thirumoola Deva Nayanar, and about the sacred history of Thirumoola Deva Nayanar. Let us contemplate them. Thirumandiram, authored by Thirumoola Deva Nayanar, is praised as Tamil Moovayiram (Three Thousand Tamil Verses). This book, Tamil Moovayiram, is named "Mantiram". Tholkappiyar states in his Sutra what a mantra is: "Niraimozhi maandhar aanaiyil kilantha maraimozhithane mantiram enba" (The secret words uttered by perfected beings by divine command are called mantras). According to this, since it was composed by Thirumoola Deva Nayanar, a perfected being, by the command of Lord Shiva's divine will, with verses that convey the meaning of the Vedas, it was called "Mantiram". It is named Thirumandiram. The book Thirumandiram has ten parts: one Payiram (preface) and nine Tantras. The Payiram section has three parts: Sirappu Payiram (special preface), Podhu Payiram (general preface), and Thatchirappu Payiram (self-special preface), comprising verses from 1 to 156. First, Sirappu Payiram. Sirappu Payiram was composed by the disciples of Thirumoola Deva Nayanar. It contains sections on the history of the Guru lineage and the greatness of the Thirumandiram collection. Next, Podhu Payiram. Podhu Payiram contains five types of invocations: to God, rain, ascetics, dharma, and crowned kings. According to the Sutra "Aayiram mugathal agandrathayinum payiram illathu panuval andre" (Even if it is vast with a thousand facets, a text without a preface is not a text), Thirumoola Deva Nayanar has provided a preface for the book he authored. In the preface, sections one, two, three, and four are invocations to God; sections five and six are invocations to ascetics; section seven is an invocation to crowned kings; section eight is an invocation to rain; and sections nine and ten are invocations to dharma. Now, the third part of the preface is Thatchirappu Payiram. Thatchirappu Payiram is the section where Thirumoola Deva Nayanar speaks to his disciples about his Guru lineage and his own history. In the section called "Kadavul Vazhthu" (invocation to God), our Thirumoola Deva Nayanar states that God is one, and that one is the one who kicked Death, the Lord worshipped by Devas as Nakkan, and His name is Shiva. He also extensively described the qualities of Lord Shiva in 50 verses. Our Thirumoola Deva Nayanar, by saying "Seelaanga vedathai seppa vandene" (I came to expound the Vedas that are the essence of sacred dance) and "Sinthai seithu Agamam seppalutrene" (I contemplated and began to expound the Agamas), has extensively elaborated on the Vedas and Agamas in the sections "Veda Sirappu" (greatness of Vedas) and "Agama Sirappu" (greatness of Agamas) with the help of the Lord's grace at that time. He has stated that there is no dharma without the Vedas, that all dharmas to be recited are in the Vedas, that scholars attained liberation by reciting the prosperous Vedas, abandoning logical debates, that Vedas and Agamas were revealed by God, that Vedas are general and Agamas are special, and that the Agamas we received through Nandiyam Peruman, such as Karanam, Kamikam, Veeram, Chintyam, Pathulam, Vyalam, Kalottaram, Suprabham, Makutam, and others, are the essence of these Agamas, which he sang as nine Tantras. Therefore, he has also stated the benefit of this book, Thirumandiram, which has a preface and nine Tantras: "Kalai ezhunthu karuthu arinthu othidin Gnalath thalaivanai nannuvar" (If one rises in the morning, understands the meaning, and recites it, one will attain the Lord of the world). Thus, the Payiram section is structured. Now, the first Tantra. This is the first Tantra of the book Thirumandiram, which is structured into nine Tantras. This section comprises Thirumandirams from 157 to 322. Thirumoola Deva Nayanar, who came to speak about the four principles starting with Gnana – Charya in Gnana, Kriya in Gnana, Yoga in Gnana, and Gnana in Gnana – first states that just as Pati (God) is beginningless among the three, Pati, Pasu (soul), and Pasam (bondage), so too are Pasu and Pasam beginningless. He states that the soul, bound by Pasam, cannot attain God by itself, but by God's grace, the impurity in the souls will subside, Gnana will shine, and the soul will experience the supreme bliss of Sivananda. He also explains how God purifies souls. To remove the impurity in souls, God, out of compassion, bestows Tanu (body), Karana (instruments), Bhuvana (worlds), and Bhoga (experiences) upon souls. When a soul experiences worldly pleasures with these Tanu and Karanas, God has revealed Vedas and Agamas to help it discern good from bad, and what is permanent from what is impermanent, so that it may attain salvation. He explains the impermanence of youth, wealth, and the body, and instructs to perform dharmas prescribed in the Vedas such as non-killing, abstaining from meat, not coveting others' wives, having love, impartiality, abstaining from alcohol, etc. He also instructs to analyze the Tattvas, arranging the 36 Tattvas as 36 steps, and to transcend all these Tattvas in a step-by-step manner. He describes the nature of Tattva Rupam, Tattva Darshanam, Tattva Suddhi, Atma Rupam, Atma Darshanam, Atma Suddhi, Shiva Rupam, Shiva Darshanam, Shiva Yogam, Shiva Bhogam, and explains that the state where the soul attains God is to realize these and transcend them step by step, entering the incomparable inner light of bliss, seeing the ineffable Shiva, and remaining in that state. He gives the example of salt dissolving in water and becoming one, and states that the Jiva will merge into Shiva like that, thus concluding this first Tantra. It is noteworthy that the dharmas stated by Thirumoola Deva Nayanar in the first Tantra are also stated by Thiruvalluva Deva Nayanar in Thirukkural, in the sections on Dharma and Wealth. Now, the second Tantra. This section comprises Thirumandirams from 323 to 530. In this second Tantra, Thirumoola Deva Nayanar emphasizes that beings should follow the path of dharma by illustrating, through Puranic stories, the benefits attained by those who follow the path of dharma and moral conduct described in the first Tantra, and the suffering experienced by those who do not. The history of Agastya, by the command of Parameswara, fighting and defeating the three cities' demons in Vindhya, Krauncha, and other places, and then going to Podigai Malai and preventing them from going north of Podigai Malai, because the rule of the three cities' demons deviated from impartiality and caused suffering to the people of the world, is narrated under the title "Agathiyam". The eight heroic deeds of Parameswara, such as the slaying of Andhakasura in Thirukkovalur, the destruction of Daksha's sacrifice in Thiruppariyalur, the severing of Brahma's head in Thirukkandiyur, the slaying of Jalandhara in Thiruvirkodi, the burning of the three cities in Thiruvathigai, the flaying of the elephant in Thiruvaluvoor, the kicking of Yama in Thirukkadavoor, and the burning of Kama in Thirukkurukkai, are narrated under the title "Pativali". The penance of Goddess Parvati, the granting of refuge to the three cities' demon kings Tarakan, Kamalakan, and Vidyunmali, the granting of life to Brahma, the discus to Vishnu, and the days to Ravana, the granting of the Chandikeswara status to Visara Sarma, the slaying of Surapadman, etc., are narrated by Thirumoola Deva Nayanar in this second Tantra under the title "Linga Puranam". The story of Damodaran, who received the discus, throwing it at Veerabhadra during Daksha's sacrifice, and Veerabhadra wearing it as one of the white skulls in his garland, and Vishnu worshipping Lord Shiva to regain the discus, is narrated under the title "Chakra Peru". Later, when Vishnu and Brahma, out of ignorance, argued that they themselves were the Lord, Paraman stood as a pillar of fire between them. Vishnu could not find the foot, and Brahma could not find the head, and they stood exhausted. The history of Lord Shiva bestowing grace upon them is narrated under the title "Adimudi Thedal". It is stated that bones and skulls are the primary cause of creation, and that if Lord Shiva did not hold bones and skulls, the five cosmic functions of creation, etc., would not take place. This is narrated under the title "Elumbum Kapalamum". The explanation of the five cosmic functions under the titles of creation, preservation, destruction, concealment, and grace, and the details of how the worlds created by Ayan, Mal, Rudran, Maheswaran, and Sadashivan, and the 84 hundred thousand types of living beings are governed, are also narrated. The method of birth of Jivas with one, two, or three impurities is narrated under the title "Karu Utpatti" (origin of embryo). Thirumoola Deva Nayanar explains in this second Tantra that if the born Jivas do not commit blasphemy against Shiva, Maheswara, or Guru, but take the help of elders, discern between worthy and unworthy recipients, and conduct themselves accordingly, they will attain the golden feet of God. The third Tantra. This section comprises mantras from 531 to 863. In this Tantra, he speaks about the eight types of Yogas: Yama, Niyama, Asana, Pranayama, Pratyahara, Dharana, Dhyana, Samadhi, and the benefits derived from them, and the eight types of Siddhis that arise. He also describes the ways to keep the body immortal and free from disease, which is the instrument for attaining Siddhis, under the title "Kayasiddhi". To make the body suitable for the Supreme Being to reside within, he first explains the necessary practices such as Varasaram, Varasoolai, Amoridharana, Ayul Parikshai, Kalachakram, etc., and then elaborates on Kesari Yoga, Paryanga Yoga, Chandra Yoga, and so on. Kesari Yoga is part of Hatha Yoga. It is very difficult, hard to practice, and not everyone can perform it. Paryanga Yoga is performed with one's spouse. It is a practice performed by a lover and beloved with unified minds and affection. This is not possible for everyone. Finding a suitable beloved is rare, rarer than rare. This is suitable for Lord Sundaramoorthy Nayanar, Paravai Ammaiyar, Sangili Ammaiyar, and for Thiruvalluva Deva Nayanar, Vasuki Ammaiyar, and others. Chandra Yoga is explained as a Yoga practiced during waxing and waning moons, knowing the 16 phases of the moon, 12 phases of the sun, 64 phases of fire, and 4 phases of stars, totaling 96 phases. He also states that among all, the eight-fold Yoga is the best. The Ashtanga Yoga described by Patanjali is different from the Sivayoga described in the Sivagamas, which can be understood through proper Guru Upadesam. Now, the fourth Tantra. This section comprises mantras from 864 to 1393. Its main parts include 13 chapters on drawing Yantras such as Ajapa (Asabai), Thiru Ambalachakram, Thirupurachakram, Bhairavi Yantram, Erolichakram, Sambhavi Mandalachakram, Bhuvanapathichakram, Navatcharichakram, etc., and also explain the five letters, Beeja mantras, etc., to be placed in the chambers of the Yantras, and the methods of worship. The Thiru Ambalachakram is embedded in the secret chamber of Chidambaram. The Thiru Ambalachakram is also present in the form of Thiyagaperuman in Thiruvarur. The Ajapa mantra is explained separately. Its details are to be revealed by the Guru to those who have received initiation. Now, the fifth Tantra. This section comprises mantras from 1394 to 1547. In this Tantra, he extensively describes the Saiva divisions, the four paths of Charya, Kriya, Yoga, and Gnana, the four margas of Sanmargam, Sagamargam, Satputra Margam, and Dasa Margam, the four states of Salokam, Samipam, Sarupam, Sayujyam, and the four levels of Mandam, Mandataram, Theeviram, Theevirataram, in 20 chapters. He states that the six religions are like the story of blind men encountering an elephant. Therefore, he explains that after seeing the six religions, understanding their principles, and becoming clear, everyone will ultimately realize that the six religious paths are flawed, and that only the path of Shiva leads to salvation, while others lead to worldly existence. This is explained by Thirumoola Deva Nayanar in this fifth Tantra. Now, the sixth Tantra. This section comprises mantras from 1548 to 1675. In this, Thirumoola Deva Nayanar describes in 14 chapters how Lord Shiva comes as a Guru to those mature individuals among humanity who are afflicted by the three impurities, removes their impurities through the Outhri Diksha (initiation) by His divine gaze, touch, word, contemplation, scripture, and yoga, gives them instruction, speaks of renunciation, makes them undertake penance, and bestows liberation. Now, the seventh Tantra. This section comprises mantras from 1676 to 2072. This Tantra contains 30 sections. Thirumoola Deva Nayanar has explained the six Adharas (psychic centers) and the Lingams such as Anda Lingam, Pinda Lingam, Sadashiva Lingam, Atma Lingam, Gnana Lingam, Shiva Lingam, from one to seven chapters, under the category of "Shiva Linga Uruva Vilakkam" (explanation of Shiva Linga form). Under the titles of Sampradayam, Thiruvarul Vaippu, Arul, Oli, Shiva Puja, he has explained Shiva Puja. Under the titles of Guru Puja, Maheswara Puja, Adiyar Perumai, Bhojana Vidhi, Bhiksha Vidhi, etc., he has explained Maheswara Puja. He states that if the body of a Gnani is burnt by fire, the whole country will burn, and if rain does not fall on earth, the world will suffer from famine, and countless kings will lose their kingdoms. Therefore, he states that the body of a Gnani should be interred in a Samadhi, and under the title "Poorana Gugai Neri Samadhi Kriyai", he explains the method of Samadhi, making a cave, and the rituals performed in Samadhi. Under the titles of Vindutpannam, Vindujayam, Bhogasara Ottam, he has described the method of semen production, controlling semen, and the way of life for those who are like "blind eyes and deaf ears" looking outwards through the fallen rod. Then, he describes the state of Aditya and the characteristics of Atmas, and clarifies the method of controlling the five senses by saying, "Anju madakkil ajadanam aam endittu anju madakka arinthene" (If one controls five, one becomes inert; thus, I knew how to control five). Then, in the last five chapters of this Tantra, he speaks about Satguru and Asatguru, condemns improper conduct, and gives ethical instructions for people to follow the good path. In this ethical instruction, the famous saying "Ondre kulamum oruvane devanum nandre niminin naman illai naaname sendre pugum gathi illenum chiththathu nindre nilapera neer ninainthuyimine" (One clan and one God; think well, there is no Yama; there is no path to enter without shame; stand firm in your mind and meditate to attain salvation) is found in this section. The famous saying "Ondre kulamum oruvane devanum" is found in this section. In this section of ethical instruction, Thirumoola Deva Nayanar has also said, "Ikkāyam nīṅki iṉi oru kāyaththil pukku piṟavāmal pōm vaḻi nāṭumiṉ ekkālaththu ivvuṭal vanthu emakku āṉathu eṉṟu akkālam uṉṉa aruḷ peṟalāmē" (Seek the way to depart from this body and not be born again in another body. When this body came to us, by contemplating that time, one can attain grace). Now, the eighth Tantra. This Tantra is composed of 33 sections, comprising mantras from 2083 to 2600. In sections one, two, and three, he speaks about the subtle body called Poriyatta Kayam, the method of transmigration of the soul from one body to another, and the different states of consciousness. In section four, he speaks about the knowledge of self. In section five, he speaks about the six ends: Vedanta, Siddhanta, Nadanta, Bodhanta, Yoganta, Kalanta. In section six, he speaks about the three categories: Pati, Pasu, Pasam, and also about light atom, material atom, life atom, etc. In section seven, he explains the meaning of Thadalai (Thal Thalai - foot and head). He clarifies by saying, "Kālum thalaiyum ariyār kalathikaḷ kāl antha sakthi aruḷ enpar kāraṇam pāloṉṟu gnānamē paṇpār thalai uyir eṉṟu kūṟi kālantha gnānaththai kāṭṭa vīṭākumē" (Those who do not know foot and head are ignorant. They say foot is the power of grace. The reason is that unified knowledge is the head, which is life. By showing that knowledge, one attains liberation). From section eight to section seventeen, he describes the three faults: Kama (desire), Veguli (anger), Mayakkam (delusion); the three words: Tat, Tvam, Asi; the three supreme states: Shivaparam, Paramparam, Tatparam; the three Turiyas: Shiva, Jiva, Para Turiyas; the three liberations: Jivanmukti, Paramukti, Shivamukti; the three forms: Aru (formless), Aruvuruvu (formless-form), Uru (form); the three Karanas: Shiva, Para, Jiva Karanas; the three voids: Mayaippal, Bodhappal, Vyomappal, and so on. In section 18, the seven Upadhis (adjuncts) of Jiva, which are effects, and the seven Upadhis of Para, which are causes, are described. In section 19, he speaks about Upashanta (tranquility). In section 20, he speaks about the method of experiencing one who has realized the inner self without speaking of the outer. In section 21, he speaks about the light that is absorbed within the Sushumna Nadi. In section 22, he speaks about the state of consciousness that arises during the vision of the non-light. In sections 23 to 27, he speaks about the way Maya is removed, the three types of Lakshanas (indicative meanings): Vitta, Vidama, Vittuvidatha, and that the Mahavakya "Tat Tvam Asi" refers to the Siddhanta conclusion, and the state where the body, freed from Maya, attains a luminous form. In sections 28 to 32, he describes truthfulness, cessation of desire, the actions of a Gnani, devotion, liberation, and so on. In section 33, he describes the omnipresent nature of God, like the sap in the sugarcane, the butter in milk, the juice in fruit, and the fragrance in a flower. He concludes this eighth Tantra by saying that the soul, like a golden creeper that seeks its beloved, will unite with God in the mind and rejoice. Now, the ninth Tantra. This section comprises mantras from 2601 to 3000. He has structured this as 22 sections, which are the essence of the Agamas. In sections one to four, he speaks about Guru Matha Darshanam, Gnana Guru Darshanam, Pranava Samadhi, and Oli (light). In sections five to seven, he explains the nature of Sthoola, Sukshma, and Ati Sukshma Panchaksharas. In section eight, he describes the five cosmic dances of God: Arputha Koothu, Thathpara Koothu, Pottrillai Koothu, Sundara Koothu, Porpathi Koothu, Sivananda Koothu, and also about Ananda Thandavam. In sections nine to sixteen, he speaks about Akasha Peru, Gnanodayam, Satya Gnananandam, Swarupa Udayam, Oozh (fate), Shiva Darshanam, Shiva Swarupa Darshanam, Mukti Bhedam, etc. In section 17, he presents a collection of verses containing hidden meanings. In sections 18 to 22, he speaks about Mona Samadhi, Varaiyarai Maatchi, Anainthor Thanmai, etc., and concludes the ideas of the Agamas by worshipping and praising our omnipresent Lord. Thus, we have briefly contemplated the essence of the ideas in the nine Tantras of the sacred text Thirumandiram, what topics these nine Tantras explain, and how they explain the ideas of the Agamas. We will continue to contemplate. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 08 — ThiruMoola Deva Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Periya Puranam, Part 907, by the divine Sekkizhar Peruman. Vambaraa Varivandu Sarukkam. Thirumoola Deva Nayanar Puranam. The commentator Sivakavimani C.K. Subramania Mudaliar Aiya explains the special features of Thirumandiram. The entire Thirumandiram is composed in a unique type of Kaliviruttha meter with four lines. This is a unique feature. Our ancestors used this meter as it is very suitable for expressing and praising the experiences of Sivananda. The beginning of the divine invocations in Periya Puranam, Kandapuranam, Kandapurana Surukkam, and Thiruvilaiyadal Puranam are all composed in this type of Viruttha poem. Kandar Anubhuti is also composed in the same meter. The name of this book is Thirumandira Malai. Dharumayadhinam Velliyambalavana Munivar Piran extensively quotes Thirumandiram verses in his Gnanaavarana Vilakka Perurai. Generally, Thirumoolar extracted the essence of Sivagamas and composed them in nine Tantras with 3000 Thiruppadalgals. Sivagamas explain the true meaning by dividing it into four paths: Charya, Kriya, Yoga, and Gnana. However, some Agamas elaborate more or less on certain sections among these. Among the 28 Sivagamas, ten, from Kamikam to Suprabhedam, are classified as Shivabheda Agamas, and eighteen, from Vijayam to Vathoolam, are classified as Rudrabheda Agamas. The names of the Kamikadi Agamas, their classifications, origins, number of verses, names of those who heard them, their Upagamas, and other details are extensively described in the Tantravatara Padalam, the first section of the Sivagamas. It is mentioned that 66 people heard the Agamas. They are the Sivarudras, from Pranavar to Mahakalar. In Kamikam Tantravatara Padalam, slokas 22 and 23, which begin with "Ishana Vatra" and "Shatshashtibhih", contain information such as 10 Shivabhedas, 18 Rudrabhedas, and 66 listeners. In Thirumandiram, the meaning of the Vedas is generally explained, and the meaning of the Sivagamas is extensively and widely expounded. "Tantra" means that which protects those who attain it. It explains the meaning of Tattva and Mantra. Tantra, Siddhanta, Agama, Mantra are all different terms that convey the same meaning. Now, these 28 Agamas have Upagamas. They are 207, from Narasimham to Visvanmakam. Due to differences in the maturity of the listeners and differences in the subject matter explained, some parts of the Sivagamas may appear to differ. However, the part that conveys the meaning in the Gnana Pada (path of knowledge) does not have differences. The Agamas are structured by the Lord in this manner, following the tradition of explaining things according to the varying degrees of impurity in souls, using the Sthula Arundhati Nyaya (gross-subtle analogy). Therefore, to resolve the confusion in the Gnana Pada of the Sivagamas, the Lord, after classifying all these Gnana Pada meanings as general and true, composed them into 12 Sutras and instructed them to Nandiyam Peruman. This is the history of Siddhanta. The details of this can be found in the special preface commentary of Sivagnana Mapadiyam and other sections. Thus, to resolve the confusion in the Gnana Pada of the Sivagamas, Nandiyam Peruman instructed the meanings he heard from the Lord to Sanatkumara and other sages. Thirumoola Deva Nayanar received initiation in that lineage. He composed the essence of the Sivagamas he heard into 3000 Thirumandirams through nine Tantras. Therefore, Thirumandiram is the distilled essence of all Sivagamas. Thus, Thirumandiram cherishes in its heart the truth of the three categories (Pati, Pasu, Pasam) spoken of in all Agamas, and the conclusions about the four paths of Charya, Kriya, Yoga, and Gnana. Among the three Vedas called "Trayi" – Rig, Yajur, Sama – the middle one is Yajur Veda. In its fourth, middle section, in the middle of the 11th Anuvaka, in the sixth Sukta, shines Sri Rudram. In its middle is Sri Panchaksharam, and in its middle is the sacred name Shiva. This is referred to as the "Aksharatvayam" (two letters) that shines in the middle of the Vedas. It is said that the Veda Purusha cherishes Shiva in his heart. Similarly, here, Thirumandiram, which is the essence of Sivagama, cherishes the truths of the three categories and the four truths starting with Gnana in its heart. How? Thirumandiram has nine Tantras. The middle one among them is the fifth Tantra. In it, Thirumoola Deva Nayanar extensively explains the four paths of Saivism, starting with Charya, and their states and types. This truth is derived because the divine Sekkizhar Peruman repeatedly emphasized it three times in the history of Thirumoola Deva Nayanar: first, in the middle, and finally, starting with "Ullunarvana Gnanam muthaliya oru nangu unmai thellu thentamizhal seppum Thirumoolar" (Thirumoolar who expounds the four truths, starting with inner wisdom, in clear sweet Tamil), and then "Gnana muthal nangu malar natthirumandira malai" (the excellent sacred garland of mantras, the four flowers starting with wisdom), and "Nalan sirantha Gnana Yoga Kriya Chariyaiyellam malarntha mozhi Thirumoola Devar" (Thirumoola Devar whose words blossomed all the excellent Gnana, Yoga, Kriya, and Charya). The fifth Tantra among the nine Tantras in Thirumandiram is the central one. The conclusion of the Gnanis is that the meaning of the four Vedas and the meaning of the Agamas are one. "Mantra" means that which protects the one who meditates on it. Mantra Upadesam (initiation into mantra) is given to those who are mature and come seeking liberation. It will be beneficial only if the listener has mental enthusiasm and determination in listening to the mantra and benefiting from it. If these are absent, it will not be beneficial. Therefore, the preface is given at the beginning to bring about these (mental enthusiasm and determination). Both Vedas and Sivagamas were created by God. Therefore, they are authoritative texts. Among these, the Vedas are general, and the Agamas are special. The Vedas are like Sutras, and the Agamas are like Bhashyas (commentaries). Thirumandiram states: "Vedamodu Agamam meyyam Iraivan nool Othum pothuvum sirappum endru unnuga" (Consider that Vedas and Agamas are the true scriptures of God, both general and special). Thirumandiram also states: "Siddhantam Vedantam kattum Sivanaiye" (Siddhanta shows the same Shiva that Vedanta shows). Therefore, here, Veda refers to the Upanishads, which are the Gnana Padas (paths of knowledge) praised as the head of the Vedas, also known as Vedanta. Among them, the Atharva Shika and other Upanishads explain the true meaning in a partial manner, following the Sthula Arundhati Nyaya, for listeners to understand according to their respective stages. Sivagamas explain the ultimate and clear meanings among them. That is why they are called Siddhanta, the ultimate conclusion. We see in Sivagnana Siddhiyar: "Vedanta thelivam Saiva Siddhanta thiran" (The essence of Vedanta is the power of Saiva Siddhanta). Sivagnana Siddhiyar states: "Veda nool Saiva nool endru irande noolgal Aadhi nool anaadhi amalan tharu nool irandum Aarana nool pothu Saivam arunjirappu noolam Neethiyinal ulagarkku sakthi nipatharkku nigazhvithathu" (There are only two types of scriptures: Vedic scriptures and Saivite scriptures. Both are primordial scriptures given by the eternal, pure Lord. Vedic scriptures are general, and Saivite scriptures are special. They were revealed for the world and for those with divine grace, according to justice). In the Puranam of Thirugnanasambandha Peruman, we saw: "Ulagiyal Veda nool ozhukkam enbathum Nilavu meinneri Sivanneri enbathum" (That the Vedic scriptures are worldly conduct, and the established true path is the path of Shiva). We also see the sacred sayings: "Vedam pasu athan pal mei Agamam" (Veda is the cow, its milk is the true Agama). If so, why are there two scriptures, Vedas and Agamas? Could they not be one? For those who have not reached the maturity to learn the special scriptures, general scriptures are needed. For those who have reached that maturity, special scriptures are needed. We also see that Vedas are for the three varnas, and Agamas are for the four varnas. We also see that the Kamikam and other Agamas, which describe the methods for Shiva Puja and other rituals prescribed in the Vedas, are indispensable. We find that Pancha Brahma, Pranava, Panchakshara, Prasada and other mantras, explanations of categories like Pati, Pasu, Pasam, applying sacred ash as Udhdhulanam and Tripundaram, wearing Rudraksha, Shiva Puja and other Shiva dharmas, and other things are found similarly in both Vedas and Sivagamas. Therefore, both are authoritative texts. Both refer to the same meaning. Lord Shiva, under the Banyan tree, instructed the four sages on the four principles of Dharma, etc., and made them attain the state of silence. From Mahendra mountain, He revealed the Sivagamas, which are the culmination of knowledge. It is said that the purpose of scriptures is to attain Dharma, Artha, Kama, and Moksha. This is a general rule for all scriptures. Some scriptures provide only one of these. The Vedas provide all four Purusharthas. Among them, after understanding the first three, one must abandon them to attain the scripture of Moksha, which then becomes beneficial. Therefore, Moksha is attained through renunciation and other causes, and in general, the Vedas provide peace to that extent. Sivagamas begin from the state of attaining peace by abandoning these, and by revealing Sivagnana, they reveal Moksha and enable seekers to attain it through practices. The Vedas, by explaining the four Purusharthas of Dharma, etc., and showing the nature of worldly life, make beings stand on the path to Moksha. We also see that Agamas, by imparting Shiva dharmas, bestow Moksha. We also see that Vedas are called worldly, and Agamas are called Shiva dharma. In Thirumandiram, we see Thirumoola Deva Nayanar speaking of the Vedas as "Vedathai vitta aram illai. Vedathul othathagum aram ellam ula" (There is no dharma without the Vedas. All dharmas to be recited are in the Vedas), and of the Agamas as "Paranai paroparam katti ulagil tharanai Siva dharmam thane sorkalam" (Showing the Supreme as transcendent, in the world, the Shiva dharma itself is the golden age). Oh good disciple, to be freed from the suffering of birth and attain liberation is the duty and action of wise people. This cannot be achieved without attaining Lord Shiva. Lord Shiva alone is Pati. There is no deity equal to Shiva, even if sought. He is older than the Trinity. He is better than a mother. Everyone worships Him, but no one is worshipped by Him. He is one whose hands have never joined in prayer. There is no other unique deity apart from Shiva's grace. Brahma, Vishnu, and others perform their respective duties by Shiva's command, by the power of authority and merit given by Him. By Shiva's grace, for the salvation of beings and to attain liberation, Lord Shiva Himself revealed the Vedas and Sivagamas. Follow their path and attain liberation. I will elaborate on their nature above. Lord Shiva instructed the Agamic principles to Nandiyam Peruman. He, in Kayilai, instructed them to Sanatkumara and other sages, and through that lineage, to me and some others. The Lord, out of grace, made me come here from Kayilai to expound those principles in Tamil for the salvation of Tamil Nadu. "Aranadi nalthorum sinthai seithu Agamam seppalutren. Thirukkooththin seelaanga vedathai seppa vanden." (I contemplated the feet of Hara daily and began to expound the Agamas. I came to expound the Vedas that are the essence of the sacred dance.) "Naan petra inbam iv vaiyagam peruvathaga" (May the joy I received be received by this world). Listen to these, follow their path, and you too attain salvation. Thus, the book begins with the instruction of Thirumoola Deva Nayanar. Therefore, this preface is given to create mental enthusiasm in the disciple for listening to the mantra initiation. Thirumandiram has nine Tantras. Among them, the fifth Tantra, which is in the middle, explains the life-giving truths of Saiva Siddhanta. Therefore, the first four Tantras, which precede the fifth Tantra, can be said to generally explain the means for a good disciple to make himself worthy, both internally and externally, to attain that Sivagnana. The four Tantras from six to nine, which follow, can be said to generally explain the benefits to be found and attained in the state of attaining Gnana. Even if milk is a good substance, if it is kept in an unclean vessel, it will spoil and become useless. Similarly, Sivagnana is good. But when it comes to one who is not pure, both internally and externally, it becomes useless and even causes harm. Therefore, one who strives to attain Gnana must first make himself worthy of it, both internally and externally. The first Tantra emphasizes this and instructs the means for it, both positively and negatively. It also mentions some other necessary things. One who is proud of his body, soul, wealth, and youth, and suffers under their influence, is not worthy of attaining Gnana. He will not have the enthusiasm to attain Gnana. Realizing the impermanence of body, wealth, and youth, and attaining a state of mind that does not cling to them even while being amidst them, like a tamarind fruit, is the first step to attaining Gnana. Therefore, in this first Tantra, Thirumoola Deva Nayanar imparts this understanding through sections like "Yakkai Nilayamai" (impermanence of the body), "Selvam Nilayamai" (impermanence of wealth), "Ilamai Nilayamai" (impermanence of youth), "Uyir Nilayamai" (impermanence of life), etc. The 145th mantra, "Oorellam koodi okka azhuthittu Perinai neekki pinam endru perittu Sooraiyam kattidai kondupoi suttittu Neerinil moozhgi ninaippu ozhinthare" (All the village gathered and cried together, removed the name and called it a corpse, took it to the cremation ground and burnt it, immersed in water and forgot), was instructed by Thirumoola Deva Nayanar to emphasize the impermanence of the body. "Mookkinil kaivaithu moodittukkondupoi Kaakkaikku kaippali kaattiyavare" (Placing hand on nose, covering it, and taking it to show as an offering to crows), and "Idappakkame irai nondhathe endrar Kidakkap paduthar kidanthu ozhinthare" (They said, 'The left side of the Lord is in pain,' laid down, and remained laid down). These are the beautiful and firm instructions given by Thirumoola Deva Nayanar. Now, the impermanence of wealth is emphasized in the 171st mantra: "Eettiya then poo manam kandirathamum Kootti konarnthoru kombidai vaithidum Ootti thurandhittu athu valiyar kola Kaatti koduthu avai kaivittavare" (The honey gathered, seeing the fragrance of flowers, brought together and placed on a branch, then abandoned it, and when strong ones took it, they showed it and gave it up). The 177th mantra also states: "Kizhakkil ezunthodiya nayiru merke Vilakkandum therar vizhi ila maandhar Kulakkanru mooththiruthay sila naalil Vilakkandum therar viyanulagore" (The sun that rose in the east runs to the west. Blind people do not understand even when they see the light. The calf of the family grows old in a few days. Even when they see the light, the people of the vast world do not understand). Thus, through these, he emphasizes the impermanence of wealth. He emphasizes the impermanence of life in the 187th mantra, saying, "Azhakkindra pothu ariyaar avarthame" (They themselves do not know when they are called). Now, merely having this feeling of impermanence is not enough. One must also refrain from evil deeds such as killing, eating meat, coveting others' wives, lust, drinking alcohol, etc. These cause harm to both body and soul simultaneously. They destroy knowledge. They do not give any room for Gnana. He emphasizes all these. He says, "Sakthiyai vendi samayathor kallunpar Sakthi izhanthu thammai marathalar" (Those of certain religions drink alcohol seeking power, but they lose power and forget themselves). He emphasizes non-killing by saying, "Patraya narkuru poosaikkum panmalar Matror anukkalai kollamai" (For the worship of the good Guru, many flowers, and not killing other beings). He emphasizes abstaining from meat by saying, "Kolaiye kalavu kal kamam poikural Malaivana pathakamaam avai neekki" (Murder, theft, alcohol, lust, lying are great sins; avoid them). He emphasizes not coveting others' wives in the 201st mantra: "Aaththa manaiyal agaththilirukkave Kaaththa manaiyalai kamurum kalaiyar Kaichcha pallavin kaniyunna maattathar Eechcham pazaththukku idarutravare" (Young men who desire another's wife while their own beloved wife is at home, are like those who cannot eat the fruit of a ripe jackfruit but suffer for a date fruit). He also speaks about poverty and harm, and the ability to prevent them. Thirumoola Deva Nayanar emphasizes that one who is afflicted by poverty will not have his mind inclined towards Gnana. He emphasizes this by saying, "Pudavai kizhithathu poyitru vazhkai Adaiyappattarum anpilar anar Kodaiyillai kolillai kondattammillai Nadaiyillai nattil iyungindrarkatke" (The cloth is torn, life is gone, those who were attained are without love, there is no giving, no taking, no celebration, no movement for those who move in the country). The importance of performing dharma is explained under the titles "Thana Sirappu" (greatness of charity) and "Aram seivanathu thiram" (the ability to do dharma). The indispensability of love and the ability of Shiva, who resides within love, to transform those who love into Shiva, are instructed by the author under the title "Anbudaimai anbu udaiyarai arivan Sivan" (Shiva knows those who possess love). The well-known Thirumandiram verse is: "Anbum Sivvum irandu enbar arivilar Anbe Sivvamavathu yaarum arikilar Anbe Sivvamavathu yaarum arinthapin Anbe Sivvamai amarndhirundhare" (The ignorant say love and Shiva are two. No one knows that love itself is Shiva. After everyone knows that love itself is Shiva, they remained as love itself, as Shiva). Therefore, although the title is "Anbe Sivam" (Love is Shiva), its meaning is "Love itself is Shiva". Thirumoola Deva Nayanar emphasizes the indispensable greatness of education and learning in three ways: "Kalvi" (education), "Kallamai" (ignorance), and "Kelvi kettu amaithal" (listening and settling). He emphasizes education by saying, "Katrarivalar karuthilor kannundu Katravar perinbamutra nindrare" (The learned have an eye in their mind. The learned stood attaining supreme bliss). He shows the loss that comes from ignorance by saying, "Kallathar inbam kaankilare" (The unlearned do not see joy), and "Ella uyirkkum Iraivane ayinum Kallathar nenjathu kana onnathe" (Though God is for all beings, He cannot be seen in the hearts of the unlearned). We see the sacred sayings: "Kallar nenjil nillan Eesan" (The Lord does not reside in the hearts of the unlearned) and "Kattravar vizhungum karpaga anniye" (The wish-fulfilling tree swallowed by the learned). Now, he shows that "Kelvi" (listening) is very much needed for a Gnana disciple. In the 300th mantra, he emphasizes the greatness of listening: "Aram kettum anthar vaaimozhi kettum Maram kettum vanavar manthiram kettum Puram kettum ponnuraimeniyam Eesan Thiram kettum petra Sivagathi thane" (Having heard dharma, having heard the words of the Brahmins, having heard the trees, having heard the mantras of the celestials, having heard the external, having heard the glory of the Lord with a golden body, one attains Shiva's path). That is, the truth of this is that education is the education that learns about Lord Shiva. Listening is the listening that listens about Lord Shiva. The education and listening spoken of in Thirukkural and other ethical texts are different. They are meant to increase general knowledge about worldly affairs. But here, this education and listening are conducive to Sivagnana concerning the nature of the soul. The topics discussed under the title "Naduvu Nilaimai" (impartiality) are also to be understood in this way. The sections "Agni Karyam" (fire ritual) and "Anthar Ozhukkam" (conduct of Brahmins) explain the disciplines necessary for attaining Gnana. It is the duty of the king to protect all these and those who strive for Gnana. Therefore, to explain this and instruct, sections like "Arasatchi Murai" (method of governance) and "Raja Raja Dosham" (faults of kings) are included. The section "Vana Sirappu" (greatness of rain) explains its indispensability because without the world being established, nothing, including Gnana, can function. For that, Gnanis also need the world to exist, and for that, rain is needed. Thirumoola Deva Nayanar structures these sections. Although these are beneficial in worldly life, the emphasis here is on their role in fostering the pursuit of Sivagnana. The conclusion of scholars is that the penance of a great ascetic cannot take place without the protection of a protector. Therefore, we can recall here the sections where the divine Sekkizhar Peruman commented on the Thiruppasuram composed by Thirugnanasambandha Peruman: "Vazhga anthar vanavar aaninam Vizhga thanpunal vendhanum onguga" (May Brahmins, celestials, and cows prosper; may cool water fall; may the king flourish), and "Ulagam inbamura santha velvigal muthal Sankararkku mun vantha archanai vazhipadu manna" (For the world to be joyful, may the worship and offerings that came before Shankara, starting with Vedic sacrifices, remain), and "Vizhpunal nalum archanai nalluruppu athalal" (Since falling water is a good part of daily worship), and "Mannanai vazhthiyathu archanai moolum matrivai kaakkum muraiyal" (Praising the king is because he protects these, which are the source of worship). Therefore, we also see here that Thirukkural emphasizes, "Anthar noorkkum arathirkkum aathiyai nindrathu mannavan kol" (The king's scepter is the origin of the Brahmin's scripture and dharma). We will continue to contemplate the thoughts on Thirumandiram. Thiruchitrambalam.
